  Test build: compiled for the STM32F412Zx, using 21 KB of flash and 15 KB of
  RAM. The output went to C:\Users\Julian\AppData\Local\Temp\claude\b4771f32385b
  2c0c\07a0c10e-7d1e-49f6-9303-6a860d965e68\scratchpad\build, so the project
  folder is unchanged.
  - The linker warned that _close, _lseek, _read and _write "are not
    implemented". There's no syscalls.c in the project. It only matters if your
    code uses printf or other stdio calls.

  Not checked yet:
  - ST-Link USB driver: I found no ST-Link driver on the machine. When you plug
    in the board, OpenOCD may not be able to open the ST-Link until a driver is
    installed. You can install ST's STSW-LINK009 driver, or bind WinUSB to it
    with Zadig. I can only confirm this with the board plugged in.
  - Programmed_Gait: this second project is a Keil MDK project (.uvprojx), and
    Keil isn't installed. It has a .ioc file, so STM32CubeMX can regenerate it
    as a Makefile project if you need it.

  To upload once the board is connected, run this from quadruped_control_mcu:
  make -j8
  openocd -f interface/stlink.cfg -f target/stm32f4x.cfg -c "program
  build/quadruped_control_nucleo.elf verify reset exit"
  You can also use the GDB steps already in your commands file and
  gdb_script.gdb, which work with this setup.

  I ran OpenOCD as a connection test only, nothing was flashed. It failed with
  libusb_open() failed with LIBUSB_ERROR_NOT_FOUND, because of the missing debug
  driver.

  To fix it, use one of these:
  1. ST's official driver (recommended): download and install STSW-LINK009 from
     st.com, then unplug and replug the board. This needs admin rights.
  2. Zadig: in the device list, pick "ST-Link Debug (Interface 0)" and install
     WinUSB. Pick only that interface. If you bind the whole composite device
     instead, you'll break COM5 and the E: drive.

  After either fix, the OpenOCD program command from my earlier reply should
  work, and so should your GDB scripts.

  You can upload now without the driver by copying the built .bin file to E:\.
  The board flashes it and resets on its own. This doesn't give you debugging or
  breakpoints, but it's enough to put firmware on the board. Do you want me to
  build and flash it that way?

  Ran 1 shell command, wrote 2 memories (ctrl+o to expand)

● I read the firmware off the board and saved it in a new firmware_backup/
  folder at the repo root. The board's flash was only read, never written. But
  one of my checks overwrote 52 bytes of the running program's RAM, so you
  should reset the board (details below).

  What's running: an Mbed OS 2 application built with the ARM Compiler, probably
  from the Mbed online compiler or Keil Studio. It is not built from
  quadruped_control_mcu: only about 3% of its bytes match my build of that
  project. I found no matching source anywhere in the repo. It's 42,496 bytes,
  uses Mbed's Serial (UART), SPI and ticker modules, and has no app-specific
  text strings. The chip had no read protection, so all 1 MB of flash was
  readable. A second full read-back matched the saved file exactly.

  What's saved:
  - The firmware: the full 1 MB flash image, plus a trimmed 42,496-byte .bin and
    a .hex to restore from.
  - Extras: option bytes, the OTP area (empty), and SHA-256 checksums.
  - README.md (firmware_backup/README.md): what the firmware is, how it was
    read, and the restore command.
  - A small .gitignore (firmware_backup/.gitignore): the repo's root .gitignore
    excludes *.bin and *.hex, so this one makes git keep these files.

  RAM side effect: the second read-back was a verify_image check, and it first
  tried a helper program that runs on the chip. OpenOCD wrote that helper into
  the first 52 bytes of RAM (0x20000000–0x20000033) without saving what was
  there, and those bytes hold the program's live variables. The CPU is still
  running and didn't crash, and the flash is untouched. Once your robot is in a
  safe state to restart, press the black RESET button on the Nucleo, and the
  program will start fresh with clean RAM. I didn't reset it myself in case
  servos are attached and would move.

  I've saved this mistake to memory so future dumps on this machine don't make
  it. I also saved the board and toolchain setup.

● I wrote a new test firmware in servo_test_leg/ that moves only the front-right
  leg. It builds with no warnings, but I haven't flashed it or run it on the
  robot yet.

  What it does:
  - After reset: it sends no servo pulses and blinks the blue LED.
  - First USER button press: it switches on the leg's three servos at their
    neutral angle, one at a time 400 ms apart. Then it loops a set movement of
    about 20 s, with smooth starts and stops. First the body/hip joint moves on
    its own (+10°, then −8°), then the femur (+20°, then −15°), then the tibia
    (±12°). Last, all three move together in a box pattern, twice.
  - Second USER press: it eases the leg back to neutral over 1 s, then stops the
    pulses.
  - Status: it prints one line per step on COM5 at 115200 baud.

  Safety:
  - The other 11 servo pins are never set up as outputs, so those servos get no
    signal.
  - Every joint is held inside the limits from your main.h: body −10/+15°, femur
    −20/+30°, tibia ±15°.
  - Pulse changes only take effect at the start of the next PWM frame, so a
    pulse is never cut short.
  - On a fault, all pulses stop and the red LED comes on.

  Where the numbers come from:
  - Wiring and timing: pins, timers and PWM timing are taken from
    quadruped_control_mcu. The front right leg is BFR on TIM2 CH1/PA15, FFR on
    TIM3 CH1/PC6, and TFR on TIM2 CH4/PB11.
  - Neutral angles: 94°, 87° and 89°. These are the open-loop mid-points from
    Programmed_Gait, which fit here because this firmware sets the PWM directly
    with no feedback.
  - Other legs: the movement and the choice of leg (TEST_LEG) are set at the top
    of Src/main.c (servo_test_leg/Src/main.c).

  Before you run it:
  - First move: as you asked, it doesn't check the leg's starting position. So
    when each servo switches on, it goes to neutral at full speed. Every move
    after that is smooth.
  - Directions: I don't know which way "+" moves each joint on the real robot.
    The sweeps go both ways, but watch the first run with a hand near the power
    switch.
  - Original firmware: flashing this replaces the Mbed firmware currently on the
    board. You can put it back from the backup with make restore.

  Build it with make and upload it with make flash from servo_test_leg/. HAL and
  CMSIS come from ../quadruped_control_mcu/Drivers; I copied only the startup
  file, linker script, system file and HAL config.

  Do you want me to flash it now? Nothing will move until you press USER.

● The test firmware is on the board and running. Flashing and verification
  passed, and the board is waiting for the USER button. No servo pulses are
  being sent until you press it.

  Checks I ran on the live board:
  - Clock: the chip runs at 96 MHz from the ST-Link's 8 MHz clock.
  - Timers: the three front-right channels are set up (TIM2 CH1 and CH4, TIM3
    CH1) with a 1 µs tick and a 3.1 ms frame. All pulse widths are 0 while it
    waits.
  - Boot message on COM5:
  === MOSAC servo test: front right leg ===
    body   servo 0 BFR  TIM2 CH1 PA15  neutral 94 deg (1171 us)  limits -10/+15
  deg
    femur  servo 4 FFR  TIM3 CH1 PC6   neutral 87 deg (1121 us)  limits -20/+30
  deg
    tibia  servo 3 TFR  TIM2 CH4 PB11  neutral 89 deg (1136 us)  limits -15/+15
  deg
    clock: 96 MHz from ST-LINK 8 MHz MCO
  Press USER (blue button) to start. Press again to park and stop.

  My first status check read all zeros. That's because the firmware puts the CPU
  to sleep between updates, and the debugger reads zeros while it sleeps.
  Reading with the CPU briefly paused gave the correct values above. I've noted
  this in memory for next time.

  To run it:
  1. Power the servos.
  2. Press the blue USER button. The three joints switch on one at a time and
     jump to neutral, then the sweeps start. Keep a hand near the servo power
     for the first run, because I don't know which way "+" moves each joint on
     the real leg.
  3. Press USER again to bring the leg back to neutral and stop the pulses.

  To follow each step, open COM5 at 115200 baud; it prints one line per step. To
  put the original firmware back, run make restore in servo_test_leg/.

> do it for the four legs

● I'll make all four legs run the same sequence at the same time by default.
  I'll also add a TEST_MODE switch to run them one leg at a time (FR, FL, BR, BL
  in turn, with the others holding neutral), and a TEST_LEGS mask to pick a
  subset. At start-up the 12 servos switch on one at a time, 250 ms apart, so
  they don't all draw their switch-on current at once.

  Ran 1 shell command · 1 failed (ctrl+o to expand)

● I couldn't upload the new four-leg firmware because the STM32 on the Nucleo
  has lost power. The build is ready in servo_test_leg/build/, but nothing was
  written to the board.

  The ST-Link side is fine: USB, COM5 and the E: drive are all still connected.
  But the ST-Link now reads the chip's supply at 0.13 V, when it was 3.25 V
  earlier today. With no power on the chip, OpenOCD can't connect.

  Your Nucleo still has the front-right-only test firmware from earlier. It will
  run that again as soon as power returns.

  The likely causes on a Nucleo-144:
  1. Power jumper: JP3, the power source selector, may be off the USB position
     (U5V or STLK). If it was moved to E5V or VIN, the board only gets power
     from that external supply, and that supply may now be off.
  2. 5V current limit tripped: if the servos are powered from the Nucleo's 5V
     pin, switching them on can exceed the USB power limit, and the board's
     protection cuts power to the chip. Servos should have their own supply,
     with only ground shared with the Nucleo. Replugging the USB cable resets
     the protection.
  3. IDD jumper: JP5 (IDD) may have been removed. That jumper carries the chip's
     3.3 V supply.

  Once it's powered again, run make flash in servo_test_leg/, or tell me and
  I'll flash it and check the boot message.

  What the new firmware does:
  - Default: all four legs run the same sequence at the same time. At start-up
    the 12 servos switch on at neutral one at a time, 250 ms apart, so they
    don't all draw their switch-on current together.
  - Settings at the top of main.c (servo_test_leg/Src/main.c): TEST_MODE =
    MODE_ONE_BY_ONE runs FR, FL, BR, BL in turn while the others hold neutral.
    TEST_LEGS picks a subset of legs.
  - Directions: the "+" direction of each joint follows the mirrored joint
    ranges in your main.h. I haven't checked that the left and right legs
    actually move as mirror images. With all legs moving, lift the robot off the
    ground for the first run.
  - Joint limits: every servo's pulse width stays well inside 500–2500 µs. The
    widest range is FFL at 907–1264 µs.

  The README (servo_test_leg/README.md) still describes the single-leg version.
  I'll update it when I flash.

> upload it now

● I uploaded the four-leg firmware. Flashing and the verify both passed. The
  board booted it and is waiting for the USER button, with no servo pulses until
  you press it.

  The boot message on COM5 confirms all 12 servos are set up:

  === MOSAC servo test: FR+FL+BR+BL, all together ===
  front right:  BFR PA15 1171us +   FFR PC6 1121us +   TFR PB11 1136us +
  front left:   BFL PC7  1171us +   FFL PC8 1121us -   TFL PC9  1164us -
  back right:   BBR PA1  1121us -   FBR PD15 1107us +  TBR PD14 1071us +
  back left:    BBL PB10 1114us -   FBL PD12 1171us -  TBL PD13 1186us -
  clock: 96 MHz from ST-LINK 8 MHz MCO

  That's shortened. The full message lists each servo's timer channel, its
  neutral angle, the number of degrees it can move, and the joint limits.

  To run it:
  1. Lift the robot off the ground, then press USER. The 12 servos switch on one
     at a time, 250 ms apart, which takes about 3 s. Then all four legs repeat
     the same sequence, about 20 s per loop.
  2. Press USER again. All legs ease back to neutral, then the pulses stop.
  3. On the first run, watch whether the left and right legs move as mirror
     images. Each servo's +/− sign comes from the joint ranges in your code, and
     I haven't checked them on the real robot. If a leg goes the wrong way,
     flip that servo's dir in the SERVOS[] table in main.c
     (servo_test_leg/Src/main.c).

  Power: if the board loses power again when the servos switch on, the cause is
  probably the servo supply (see my last message). The servos need their own
  supply, sharing only ground with the Nucleo.

  I've updated the README (servo_test_leg/README.md) for the four-leg version.
  It now includes the full pin table and how to switch to one leg at a time.

      383 +/* =============================== Feedback ==================
          +============ */                                               
      384 +                                                              
      385 +/* ADC code -> degrees, linear interpolation over the mean of 
          +the down/up tables. */                                        
      386 +static float adc_to_deg(int n, float code)                    
      387 +{                                                             
      388 +    const float (*t)[MED_CALIB] = CALIB_TABLE[n];             
      389 +    float prev_code = 0.0f, prev_deg = 0.0f;                  
      390 +    for (int k = 0; k < MED_CALIB; k++) {                     
      391 +        float c = (t[0][k] + t[1][k]) * 0.5f / MEAS_PER_ANG;  
      392 +        if (code <= c) {                                      
      393 +            if (k == 0) return ANGLES[0];                     
      394 +            return prev_deg + (ANGLES[k] - prev_deg) * (code -
          + prev_code) / (c - prev_code);                                
      395 +        }                                                     
      396 +        prev_code = c;                                        
      397 +        prev_deg  = ANGLES[k];                                
      398 +    }                                                         
      399 +    return MAX_TABLE_ANGLE;                                   
      400 +}                                                             
      401 +                                                              
      402 +static uint16_t median_of_channel(int n)                      
      403 +{                                                             
      404 +    uint16_t v[ADC_SCANS];                                    
      405 +    for (int i = 0; i < ADC_SCANS; i++) v[i] = adc_buf[i][n]; 
      406 +    for (int i = 1; i < ADC_SCANS; i++) {            /* insert
          +ion sort */                                                   
      407 +        uint16_t x = v[i];                                    
      408 +        int k = i - 1;                                        
      409 +        while (k >= 0 && v[k] > x) { v[k + 1] = v[k]; k--; }  
      410 +        v[k + 1] = x;                                         
      411 +    }                                                         
      412 +    return (uint16_t)((v[ADC_SCANS / 2 - 1] + v[ADC_SCANS / 2]
          + + 1u) / 2u);                                                 
      413 +}                                                             
      414 +                                                              
      415 +static void feedback_update(void)                             
      416 +{                                                             
      417 +    for (int n = 0; n < 12; n++) {                            
      418 +        fb_raw[n] = median_of_channel(n);                     
      419 +        fb_deg[n] = adc_to_deg(n, fb_raw[n]);                 
      420 +    }                                                         
      421 +}                                                             
      422 +                                                              
      423 +/* <cmd>/<meas>/<err> in degrees; "off" when the servo gets no
          + pulses. */                                                   
      424 +static void print_joint_fb(int l, int j)                      
      425 +{                                                             
      426 +    uint8_t n = LEGS[l].servo[j];                             
      427 +    if (joint_on[l][j]) {                                     
      428 +        float cmd = joint_cmd_deg(l, j, pose[l][j]);          
      429 +        uart_dec1(cmd);                                       
      430 +        uart_puts("/");                                       
      431 +        uart_dec1(fb_deg[n]);                                 
      432 +        uart_puts("/");                                       
      433 +        uart_dec1(fb_deg[n] - cmd);                           
      434 +    } else {                                                  
      435 +        uart_puts("off/");                                    
      436 +        uart_dec1(fb_deg[n]);                                 
      437 +        uart_puts("/-");                                      
      438 +    }                                                         
      439 +}                                                             
      440 +                                                              
      441 +static void print_telemetry(uint32_t now)                     
      442 +{                                                             
      443 +    uart_puts("fb ");                                         
      444 +    uart_dec1((float)now / 1000.0f);                          
      445 +    for (int l = 0; l < LEG_COUNT; l++) {                     
      446 +        if (!leg_selected(l)) continue;                       
      447 +        uart_puts(" ");                                       
      448 +        uart_puts(LEGS[l].tag);                               
      449 +        for (int j = 0; j < JOINTS_PER_LEG; j++) {            
      450 +            uart_puts(" ");                                   
      451 +            print_joint_fb(l, j);                             
      452 +        }                                                     
      453 +        uart_puts(" adc");                                    
      454 +        for (int j = 0; j < JOINTS_PER_LEG; j++) {            
      455 +            uart_puts(j ? "/" : " ");                         
      456 +            uart_int(fb_raw[LEGS[l].servo[j]], false);        
      457 +        }                                                     
      458 +    }                                                         
      459 +    if (print_all_raw) {                                      
      460 +        uart_puts(" all");                                    
      461 +        for (int n = 0; n < 12; n++) {                        
      462 +            uart_puts(" ");                                   
      463 +            uart_int(fb_raw[n], false);                       
      464 +        }                                                     
      465 +    }                                                         
      466 +    uart_puts("\r\n");                                        
      467 +}

      469 +static void update_tracking_stats(void)                       
      470 +{                                                             
      471 +    for (int l = 0; l < LEG_COUNT; l++) {                     
      472 +        if (!leg_selected(l)) continue;                       
      473 +        for (int j = 0; j < JOINTS_PER_LEG; j++) {            
      474 +            if (!joint_on[l][j]) continue;                    
      475 +            float e = absf(fb_deg[LEGS[l].servo[j]] - joint_cm
          +d_deg(l, j, pose[l][j]));                                     
      476 +            if (e > stats[l][j].track_max) stats[l][j].track_m
          +ax = e;

  Ran 1 shell command (ctrl+o to expand)

● It builds clean at 17.9 KB, so I'm flashing it and running the serial test: a
  rest recording, one full front-right leg cycle, then parking the leg, with the

  Ran 1 shell command · 1 failed (ctrl+o to expand)

● The flash failed again with low target voltage, so nothing was written and the

  Ran 1 shell command · 1 failed (ctrl+o to expand)

● I couldn't run the front-right test because the STM32 has lost power again.
  This is the second time today. The ST-Link reads 0.12 V on the chip and can't
  connect, while the ST-Link itself (USB, COM5) is still connected. Nothing was
  flashed and the leg didn't move.

  The new firmware is built and ready in servo_test_leg/build/ (17.9 KB, no
  warnings). It does what you asked:
  - Front right only: TEST_LEGS is LEG_BIT(LEG_FR). The other 11 servos get no
    PWM.
  - Motor feedback: it reads all 12 servo pots on the ADC and converts each one
    to degrees using your calibration_ADC.h tables. The front-right pots are BFR
    on PA4, FFR on PC4 and TFR on PC1, the same channel order as
    quadruped_control_mcu.
  - Live feedback output: every 100 ms it prints the commanded angle, measured
    angle and error for each joint, plus the raw ADC values. It keeps printing
    even with the servos off, so you can move the leg by hand to check that the
    pots respond.
  - Accuracy summary: each extreme now has a 500 ms hold. At the end of each
    hold it logs the settled error. After each round it prints the mean and max
    settled error and the max tracking error for each joint.
  - Serial control: s starts, x stops and a adds the raw ADC values of all 12
    channels. That's how I'll run the test and record the feedback from here.

  The power loss: both drops happened while the test firmware was on the board.
  Did they happen when you pressed USER? If so, the servos are almost certainly
  dragging down the Nucleo's supply when they switch on and start moving. If
  they're powered from the Nucleo's 5V pin, or the Nucleo runs from the servo
  supply through JP3 = E5V/VIN, the board's current limiting cuts power to the
  chip. Twelve servos switching on would explain this time. The fix is a
  separate supply for the servos, with only ground shared with the Nucleo.

  When the board has power again, tell me and I'll flash it and run the test.
  Don't press USER yourself; I'll start it with s. I'll then report the measured
  angles against the commanded ones for each joint, and whether each pot moves
  only when its own joint moves.

> try again

  ⎿  Added 47 lines, removed 38 lines
       1  # servo_test_leg
       2 
       3 -A small test firmware for the NUCLEO-F412ZG. It moves the legs 
         -through a fixed sequence of joint sweeps. By default all four l
         -egs run the sequence at the same time. It runs open loop: it do
         -esn't use the ADC/pot feedback, the Raspberry Pi or the SPI lin
         -k. Only the PWM pins of the selected legs are configured. Servo
         -s on any other leg get no signal.                              
       3 +A small test firmware for the NUCLEO-F412ZG. It moves the selec
         +ted legs (default: **front right only**) through a fixed sequen
         +ce of joint sweeps and reads each servo's pot feedback. It runs
         + open loop: it doesn't use the Raspberry Pi or the SPI link. Th
         +e feedback is only measured and reported; it is not used to con
         +trol the servos. Only the PWM pins of the selected legs are con
         +figured. Servos on any other leg get no signal.                
       4 
       5  ## Behaviour
       6 
       7  | State | What happens | LED |
       8  |---|---|---|

      26 -Run these from this folder:                                    
      26 +ADC1 scans all 12 pot inputs continuously over DMA. It uses the
         + same channel order as `quadruped_control_mcu`, where rank n+1 
         +is servo n, with 480-cycle samples. Each channel is the median 
         +of the last 64 scans, about 16 ms. It is converted to degrees w
         +ith the tables in `quadruped_control_mcu/Core/Inc/calibration_A
         +DC.h`, using the mean of the "down" and "up" tables. These tabl
         +es use the same angle scale as the PWM command, so measured min
         +us commanded is the error.                                     
      27 
      28 +The channel order was checked on the front-right leg: each pot 
         +only changed when its own joint moved. The `IN4`/`IN11` labels 
         +in the comments of `calibration_ADC.h` are out of date.

      60  All the settings are at the top of `Src/main.c`:
      61 
      51 -- `TEST_LEGS`: which legs to drive. It's a bit mask, for exampl
         -e `LEG_BIT(LEG_FR)` or `LEG_BIT(LEG_FR) | LEG_BIT(LEG_BL)`. The
         - default is `ALL_LEGS`.                                        
      52 -- `TEST_MODE`: `MODE_TOGETHER` (the default) or `MODE_ONE_BY_ON
         -E`. In one-by-one mode, FR, FL, BR and BL run the sequence in t
         -urn while the others hold neutral.                             
      53 -- `SEQUENCE[]`: the movement. Each line is the time to reach th
         -e pose in ms, followed by the body, femur and tibia offsets.   
      54 -- `TIME_SCALE_PCT`: slows down or speeds up the whole sequence.
      55 -- `SEQUENCE_REPEATS`: how many rounds to run before parking. 0 
         -means forever.                                                 
      56 -- `JOINT_LIMIT_DEG`: the safety clamp.                         
      62 +- `TEST_LEGS`: which legs to drive. It's a bit mask: `LEG_BIT(L
         +EG_FR)` (the default), `ALL_LEGS`, or a combination such as `LE
         +G_BIT(LEG_FR) | LEG_BIT(LEG_BL)`.                              
      63 +- `TEST_MODE`: `MODE_TOGETHER` or `MODE_ONE_BY_ONE`. In one-by-
         +one mode, FR, FL, BR and BL run the sequence in turn while the 
         +others hold neutral.                                           
      64 +- `SEQUENCE[]`: the movement. Each line is the time to reach th
         +e pose in ms, followed by the body, femur and tibia offsets. Re
         +peat a pose to hold it.                                        
      65 +- `TIME_SCALE_PCT`, `SEQUENCE_REPEATS`, `JOINT_LIMIT_DEG`, `TEL
         +EMETRY_MS`, `ADC_SCANS`.

  What the results show:
  - The ADC channels are wired as the code expects. When one joint moved on its
    own, the other two pots stayed within about 0.5°. The IN4/IN11 labels in the
    calibration_ADC.h comments are out of date; the channel order in the code
    is correct.
  - The measured angle is consistently about 2.4° below the command. It's the
    same whether the joint approached from above or below, so it isn't servo
    slack (that would flip sign with direction). It looks like a calibration
    shift. Two possible causes:
    - The ADC sample time: I sample for 480 cycles and your calibration used 84.
    - The supply voltage feeding the servos' pots may have changed since you
      calibrated.

    The joints only moved about ±20°, which isn't enough to tell a fixed offset
    from a small scale error. This matters for quadruped_control_mcu: its PID
    would push every joint about 2.4° further than the open-loop angle.
  - The first move at switch-on is large. Before switching on, the pots read BFR
    +18°, FFR +59° and TFR −26° from neutral. The femur covered those 59° in
    about 0.25 s. While it moved, the unpowered tibia servo was turned about 22°
    by the leg's linkage.
  - The error while moving is about 2° on top of the offset, which is the servo
    lagging behind the command.

  I've updated the README (servo_test_leg/README.md) to cover the feedback
  output and the serial commands. The full log is at
  C:\Users\Julian\AppData\Local\Temp\claude\b4771f32385b2c0c\07a0c10e-7d1e-49f6-
  9303-6a860d965e68\scratchpad\fr_feedback_run1.log.

  Next steps (each one moves the leg again):
  1. Soft start: read the pots before switching on, start each servo at its
     measured angle, and ease it to neutral. That replaces the 59° jump with one
     of about 2–3°.
  2. Check the offset: rerun with the same 84-cycle ADC sampling as your
     calibration, to see whether the 2.4° goes away.

● The movement is a list of 29 keyframes in main.c:89-124
  (servo_test_leg/Src/main.c#L89-L124). One loop takes about 25 s and then
  repeats. Each keyframe gives a target angle for each joint, measured from its
  neutral position, and the time to reach it.

  Every move is eased, so it starts and ends at zero speed. The fastest any
  joint moves is about 47°/s.

  Before the loop: the servos switch on at neutral one at a time: BFR at 0 ms,
  FFR at 250 ms, TFR at 500 ms. The loop starts at 750 ms.

  The front right leg's neutral angles are body (BFR) 94°, femur (FFR) 87° and
  tibia (TFR) 89°.

  Phases 1–3 sweep each joint on its own, first in the + direction and then the
  − direction, then back to neutral. Each extreme is held for 0.5 s so the
  feedback can settle. Moving one joint at a time is also what showed that each
  pot belongs to the right joint.

  Phase 4 moves all three joints at once. The femur and tibia trace a rectangle:
  femur +15/−10 and tibia ±10. The body rocks ±5° at the same time. It goes
  around the rectangle twice.

  Limits: every target stays inside the joint limits (body −10/+15°, femur
  −20/+30°, tibia ±15°). Anything outside them would be clamped.

  Direction: I don't know which physical direction "+" is on the robot, for
  example whether femur +20 lifts or lowers the leg.

  Changing it: to change the movement, edit the rows of SEQUENCE[]. Each row is
  { time_ms, { body, femur, tibia } }, and repeating a pose makes a hold. To
  slow the whole loop down, set TIME_SCALE_PCT to 200, which makes it twice as
  slow.

  The chip is unpowered now (0.12 V), so the servos get no signal and won't
  move. When power returns the firmware boots and waits. Don't press USER yet.

  What the feedback showed:
  - Femur (FFL) stalled: it was told to go to 87° and stopped at about 107.5°
    for over 2 s. A stalled servo draws close to its maximum current the whole
    time. The front-right femur reached the same 87° without trouble.
  - Hip (BFL) shook ±10°: it was told to stay at 94° but swung between 84° and
    107° about 2–3 times a second. The front-right hip held steady to within
    ±0.14°.
  - Power dip at switch-on: when BFL switched on, its pot reading dropped to
    nearly 0 V for a moment.
  - Just before the dropout: all three pot readings shifted down by 2.5–24° at
    the same instant. When every channel moves together like that, it means the
    supply or ground was shifting, not the joints. Then the board went dead.

  What I think happened: the stalled femur pulled a lot of current, the servo
  supply sagged, the hip servo started to shake because its voltage dropped, and
  finally the Nucleo browned out. The front-right run had no stall, and the
  power held for the whole round. That points strongly at the servo power
  supply, not the firmware.

  Before testing this leg again:
  1. Check the front-left femur by hand: with power off, is it free to move to
     the neutral position, or does the leg hit the body, the ground or a hard
     stop? Lift the robot off the ground in any case.
  2. Check the power wiring: the servo supply needs to handle several amps when
     servos stall. The Nucleo shouldn't be powered from that same supply, either
     through its 5V pin or through the JP3 power jumper set to E5V/VIN.
  3. Stall cut-out (I recommend this): I can add a cut-out to the firmware. If a
     joint stays more than about 10° from its target for 0.5 s, or the
  ⎿  Interrupted · What should Claude do instead?

> it abruptly moved to the top position very violetly and suddenly

  Ran 1 shell command, wrote 1 memory (ctrl+o to expand)

● That violent move was caused by my firmware design. When a servo switched on,
  it was sent straight to a fixed "neutral" angle at full speed, wherever it
  happened to be. On the front-left leg, that angle (87° for the femur, taken
  from Programmed_Gait) is beyond the leg's mechanical range. The femur slammed
  upward into its top stop at about 107.5° and stayed jammed there, drawing
  stall current. That's very likely why the board lost power 2 s later.

  Right now it's safe: the Nucleo is unpowered, so the servos get no signal. But
  the firmware on the board is still the old version. Don't press USER when
  power comes back, or the same jump will happen again.

  I've fixed the firmware. It's built but not flashed yet, because the board has
  no power:
  - Soft start: each servo's first pulse is at the angle its pot reads, and it
    then eases to neutral at about 20°/s. Nothing jumps any more.
  - Stall cut-out: if any joint stays more than 8° from its command for 250 ms,
    all pulses switch off and the red LED comes on. In the front-left run it
    would have cut out within about half a second, not after more than 2 s of
    stalling.
  - Pot check: it refuses to start if a pot reading is out of range or noisy, so
    a disconnected pot can't send a servo to a random angle.

  The front-left femur's neutral angle is still wrong. With the fix, that leg
  would ease up to the stop gently and then cut out, but it still wouldn't work.
  I'd rather find the leg's real neutral before testing it again.

  The safest way to find it: once power is back, I flash the new firmware, which
  sends no pulses until started. Then you move the front-left leg by hand, with
  no power to the servos, to its normal standing position and through its full
  range. I record the pot readings and set FL's neutral angle and limits from
  them. Shall we do that? Or if you already know which position "neutral" should
  be for this leg, tell me.

  I've also updated the README (servo_test_leg/README.md) and noted in memory
  never to start servos with a jump again.

> rewrite it so it moves slowly and no violent movements

● I'll rewrite the motion engine so every move is slow and gentle by design:
  - Speed: every move is planned from a maximum joint speed of 10°/s, not from a
    fixed time. The sequence keeps the same poses and simply takes longer.
  - Hard limit: a slew limit of 15°/s on every pulse width means no command can
    ever jump, even if something else goes wrong.
  - Start-up: servos switch on one at a time, each at the angle its pot reads.
    The firmware learns each pot's offset during a 400 ms hold, and it aborts
    the start if a servo doesn't follow its first command.
  - Fault cut-out: an averaged error check catches stalls, blocked joints and
    oscillation, and cuts all pulses within about 0.5 s.
  ⎿  Interrupted · What should Claude do instead?

> continue

      460 +/* Switches a joint on at the angle its pot reads now: no jump

      461 +static void joint_enable(int l, int j)                        
      462 +{                                                             
      463 +    float now_deg = fb_deg[servo_of(l, j)];                   
      464 +    cmd_deg[l][j]      = now_deg;                             
      465 +    out_deg[l][j]      = now_deg;                             
      466 +    offset_deg[l][j]   = 0.0f;                                
      467 +    offset_known[l][j] = false;                               
      468 +    err_avg[l][j]      = 0.0f;

      470 +    set_pulse(l, j, deg_to_us(now_deg));                      
      471 +}

      473 +static void outputs_off(void)                                 
      474 +{

      478 +            joint_on[l][j]     = false;                       
      479 +            offset_known[l][j] = false;

      482 +    }

      484 +                                                              
      485 +/* ============================ Motion planning ==============
          +============ */                                               
      486 +                                                              
      487 +static float ease(float t)  /* smoothstep: zero speed at both 
          +ends, peak 1.5x mean */                                       
      488 +{                                                             
      489 +    if (t <= 0.0f) return 0.0f;                               
      490 +    if (t >= 1.0f) return 1.0f;                               
      491 +    return t * t * (3.0f - 2.0f * t);                         
      492 +}                                                             
      493 +                                                              
      494 +/* Plans a move from the current command to `to`. Its time is 
          +set so that the                                               
      495 + * joint moving furthest peaks at MAX_SPEED_DEG_S. */         
      496 +static void segment_plan(segment_t *sg, const float to[LEG_COU
          +NT][JOINTS_PER_LEG],                                          
      497 +                         uint32_t hold_ms, uint32_t now)      
      498 +{                                                             
      499 +    float max_delta = 0.0f;                                   
      500 +    memcpy(sg->from, cmd_deg, sizeof sg->from);               
      501 +    memcpy(sg->to, to, sizeof sg->to);                        
      502 +    for (int l = 0; l < LEG_COUNT; l++) {                     
      503 +        if (!leg_selected(l)) continue;                       
      504 +        for (int j = 0; j < JOINTS_PER_LEG; j++) {            
      505 +            float d = absf(sg->to[l][j] - sg->from[l][j]);    
      506 +            if (d > max_delta) max_delta = d;                 
      507 +        }                                                     
      508 +    }                                                         
      509 +    sg->move_ms = 0;                                          
      510 +    if (max_delta > 0.05f) {                                  
      511 +        sg->move_ms = (uint32_t)(1.5f * max_delta / MAX_SPEED_
          +DEG_S * 1000.0f);                                             
      512 +        if (sg->move_ms < MIN_MOVE_MS) sg->move_ms = MIN_MOVE_
          +MS;                                                           
      513 +    }                                                         
      514 +    sg->hold_ms  = hold_ms;                                   
      515 +    sg->start_ms = now;                                       
      516 +}                                                             
      517 +                                                              
      518 +/* Updates the planned command; returns true once the move and
          + the hold are done. */                                        
      519 +static bool segment_step(const segment_t *sg, uint32_t now)   
      520 +{                                                             
      521 +    uint32_t elapsed = now - sg->start_ms;                    
      522 +    float k = sg->move_ms ? ease((float)elapsed / (float)sg->m
          +ove_ms) : 1.0f;                                               
      523 +    for (int l = 0; l < LEG_COUNT; l++)                       
      524 +        for (int j = 0; j < JOINTS_PER_LEG; j++)              
      525 +            cmd_deg[l][j] = sg->from[l][j] + (sg->to[l][j] - s
          +g->from[l][j]) * k;                                           
      526 +    return elapsed >= sg->move_ms + sg->hold_ms;              
      527 +}                                                             
      528 +                                                              
      529 +static void neutral_targets(float to[LEG_COUNT][JOINTS_PER_LEG
          +])                                                            
      530 +{                                                             
      531 +    for (int l = 0; l < LEG_COUNT; l++)                       
      532 +        for (int j = 0; j < JOINTS_PER_LEG; j++)              
      533 +            to[l][j] = SERVOS[servo_of(l, j)].neutral_deg;    
      534 +}                                                             
      535 +                                                              
      536 +static uint8_t moving_legs(int current_leg)                   
      537 +{                                                             
      538 +    return TEST_MODE == MODE_ONE_BY_ONE ? LEG_BIT(current_leg)
          + : TEST_LEGS;                                                 
      539 +}

       1  # servo_test_leg
       2 
       3 -A small test firmware for the NUCLEO-F412ZG. It moves the selec
         -ted legs (default: **front right only**) through a fixed sequen
         -ce of joint sweeps and reads each servo's pot feedback. It runs
         - open loop: it doesn't use the Raspberry Pi or the SPI link. Th
         -e feedback is only measured and reported; it is not used to con
         -trol the servos. Only the PWM pins of the selected legs are con
         -figured. Servos on any other leg get no signal.                
       3 +A small test firmware for the NUCLEO-F412ZG. It moves the selec
         +ted legs **slowly** through a fixed sequence of joint sweeps an
         +d reads each servo's pot feedback. It runs open loop: it doesn'
         +t use the Raspberry Pi or the SPI link. The feedback is used to
         + start without jumps and to stop on faults, not to control the 
         +servos. Only the PWM pins of the selected legs are configured. 
         +Servos on any other leg get no signal.                         
       4 
       5 +## Motion safety                                               
       6 +                                                               
       7 +- **Slow, eased moves.** Every move is planned from a speed lim
         +it instead of a fixed time. The joint that moves furthest peaks
         + at `MAX_SPEED_DEG_S` (10°/s), and every move starts and ends a
         +t zero speed.                                                  
       8 +- **Hard slew limit.** No pulse width can change faster than `M
         +AX_SLEW_DEG_S` (15°/s), whatever the planner asks for. A comman
         +d can never jump.                                              
       9 +- **Start without jumps.** Servos switch on one at a time. Each
         + one starts at the angle its pot reads at that moment, so its f
         +irst pulse hardly moves it: only the pot offset, about 2–3°. Th
         +e servo holds there for 400 ms while the pot offset is learned.
         + If a servo ends up more than 5° away, the start is aborted. Th
         +en all joints ease to neutral at the speed limit.              
      10 +- **Fault cut-out.** If a powered joint's averaged error (after
         + the pot offset) goes above `FAULT_ERR_DEG` (4°, averaged over 
         +about 100 ms), all pulses are cut. That covers blocked, stalled
         + and oscillating joints.                                       
      11 +- **Pot check.** It refuses to start if a pot reading is out of
         + range or noisy. That catches a disconnected or shorted pot.   
      12 +                                                               
      13  ## Behaviour
      14 
      15  | State | What happens | LED |
      16  |---|---|---|
       9 -| After reset | No servo pulses. Feedback is still streamed, so
         - you can move a joint by hand to check its pot. | Blue blinking
         - |                                                             
      10 -| **USER** pressed or `s` sent | Soft start: each servo's pulse
         -s begin at the angle its pot reads now, then ease to neutral at
         - about 20°/s. It refuses to start if a pot reading looks invali
         -d (out of range or noisy). Then the sequence loops. | Green on 
         -|                                                              
      11 -| **USER** pressed or `x` sent | All legs ease back to neutral 
         -over 1 s, then the pulses stop | Green flashing, then off |    
      12 -| Stall | A powered joint stays more than 8° from its command f
         -or 250 ms. All pulses are cut. | Red on |                      
      17 +| After reset | No servo pulses. Feedback is streamed, so you c
         +an move a joint by hand to check its pot. | Blue blinking |    
      18 +| **USER** pressed or `s` sent | Switch-on and easing to neutra
         +l as above, then the sequence loops | Green on |               
      19 +| **USER** pressed or `x` sent | Eases slowly back to neutral, 
         +holds 0.3 s, then the pulses stop | Green flashing, then off | 
      20  | Fault | All pulses stop | Red on |
      21 
      15 -Nothing jumps. The soft start begins at the measured position, 
         -and every move after that is eased: it starts and ends at zero 
         -speed. Earlier versions jumped to neutral at full speed from wh
         -erever the joint was. On the front-left leg that slammed the fe
         -mur into its top stop. **When all legs move, lift the robot off
         - the ground.**                                                 
      22 +**When all legs move, lift the robot off the ground.**         
      23 
      17 -**Front left leg:** its femur neutral (87°, from `Programmed_Ga
         -it`) seems to be outside the leg's mechanical range. In the fir
         -st test the femur stopped at 107.5° against its top stop. Check
         - the FL neutral angles before you test that leg again.         
      24 +**Front left leg:** its femur neutral (87°, from `Programmed_Ga
         +it`) seems to be outside the leg's mechanical range. In an earl
         +ier version, which jumped, the femur slammed into its top stop 
         +at 107.5°. With this version it would ease slowly towards the s
         +top and the fault cut-out would switch it off. Check the FL neu
         +tral angles before you test that leg.                          
      25 
      19 -The sequence takes about 25 s per loop. Each extreme has a 500 
         -ms hold, and the feedback is logged as "settled" at the end of 
         -each hold.                                                     
      20 -1. The body (hip) joint moves alone: +10°, then −8°.           
      21 -2. The femur moves alone: +20°, then −15°.

      41  ## Feedback
      42 
      29 -ADC1 scans all 12 pot inputs continuously over DMA. It uses the
         - same channel order as `quadruped_control_mcu`, where rank n+1 
         -is servo n, with 480-cycle samples. Each channel is the median 
         -of the last 64 scans, about 16 ms. It is converted to degrees w
         -ith the tables in `quadruped_control_mcu/Core/Inc/calibration_A
         -DC.h`, using the mean of the "down" and "up" tables. These tabl
         -es use the same angle scale as the PWM command, so measured min
         -us commanded is the error.                                     
      43 +ADC1 scans all 12 pot inputs continuously over DMA. It uses the
         + same channel order as `quadruped_control_mcu`, where rank n+1 
         +is servo n, with 480-cycle samples. Each channel is the median

         +DC.h`, using the mean of the "down" and "up" tables. The channe
         +l order was checked on the front-right leg: each pot only chang
         +ed when its own joint moved. The `IN4`/`IN11` labels in the com

      48 
      49  The ST-LINK virtual COM port runs at 115200 8N1. On this PC it
          is COM5. Output is interrupt-driven, so it never slows down the
           motion.
      50 
      51  ```
      38 -fb 12.3 FR 104.0/101.2/-2.8 87.0/84.1/-2.9 89.0/86.0/-3.0 adc 1
         -486/1257/1248                                                  
      39 -step 8/29  FR  body  +0  femur +20  tibia +0 deg  in 1000 ms  -
         -> FR 1171/1264/1136 us                                         
      40 -settled step 9 FR BFR 94.0/91.6/-2.4 FFR 107.0/104.8/-2.2 TFR 8
         -9.0/86.8/-2.2

      42 -  BFR  settled mean 2.3 max 2.8 (11 holds)  tracking max 4.0   
      52 +  FFL on at 123.2 deg, pot offset -2.4 deg                     
      53 +step 5/19  FL  body  +0  femur +20  tibia +0 deg  move 3000 ms

      54 +fb 12.3 FL 94.0/91.6/0.0 72.1/69.8/0.1 93.0/90.6/0.0 adc 1341/1
         +060/1320

         +0/90.7/0.1

         -e body, femur and tibia the commanded, measured and error value
         -s in degrees, then the raw ADC codes. A joint without pulses sh
         -ows `off/<meas>/-`.                                            
      46 -- `settled` lines are logged at the end of each hold. `stats` l
         -ines come after each round and when you stop.

      65 -- `TEST_LEGS`: which legs to drive. It's a bit mask: `LEG_BIT(L
         -EG_FR)` (the default), `ALL_LEGS`, or a combination such as `LE
         -G_BIT(LEG_FR) | LEG_BIT(LEG_BL)`.                              
      78 +- `TEST_LEGS`: which legs to drive, as a bit mask, for example 
         +`LEG_BIT(LEG_FR)`, `ALL_LEGS`, or `LEG_BIT(LEG_FR) | LEG_BIT(LE
         +G_BL)`.                                                        
      79  - `TEST_MODE`: `MODE_TOGETHER` or `MODE_ONE_BY_ONE`. In one-by-
          one mode, FR, FL, BR and BL run the sequence in turn while the
          others hold neutral.
      67 -- `SEQUENCE[]`: the movement. Each line is the time to reach th
         -e pose in ms, followed by the body, femur and tibia offsets. Re
         -peat a pose to hold it.

         -EMETRY_MS`, `ADC_SCANS`.                                       
      80 +- `MAX_SPEED_DEG_S`: how slowly everything moves. `MAX_SLEW_DEG
         +_S` must stay above it.                                        
      81 +- `SEQUENCE[]`: the movement. Each line is the pose in degrees 
         +from neutral (body, femur, tibia), followed by the hold time in
         + ms. Move times are worked out from the speed limit.

         +`, `SEQUENCE_REPEATS`, `JOINT_LIMIT_DEG`.

● I've rewritten the firmware so every movement is slow and gentle. It builds
  with no warnings, but I couldn't flash it: the board still has no power (0.12
  V). The old firmware that jumps is still on the board, so don't press USER
  until I've flashed this version.

  What changed:
  - Slow moves: each move's duration now comes from a speed limit, not a fixed
    time. The joint moving furthest peaks at 10°/s and every move starts and
    ends gently. Before, moves peaked at about 47°/s and the switch-on jumps
    were at the servo's full speed. One round of the same sequence now takes
    about 57 s.
  - A hard speed cap: whatever the motion planner asks for, no servo command can
    change faster than 15°/s.
  - Switching on without a jump: servos switch on one at a time, each at the
    angle its pot reads at that moment. Only the pot offset of about 2–3° shows
    as a small settle. Each servo holds for 400 ms while the firmware learns
    that offset. If a servo doesn't stay where it was told, the start is
    aborted. Then the leg eases to neutral at 10°/s.
  - Stopping: the leg eases back to neutral at the same slow speed, then the
    pulses stop.
  - Fault cut-out: if any joint's error stays above 4° on average, all pulses
    are cut and the red LED comes on. That catches a blocked or stalled joint
    and the kind of shaking BFL showed, typically within about half a second.
    Slow moves also draw less current, which should help with the power
    dropouts.

  Front left is still not ready to run. TEST_LEGS is still set to front left,
  and its femur neutral (87°) appears to be beyond the leg's top stop. With this
  version it would ease slowly into the stop and the fault cut-out would switch
  it off, so no slam, but the test still wouldn't complete.

  When the board has power again, tell me and I'll flash it. It will wait
  without moving. Then either:
  1. Find the right front-left neutral: with the servos unpowered, you move the
     front-left leg by hand to its standing pose while I log the pots, and I set
     its neutral and limits from that. I recommend this before running front
     left again.
  2. Run front right first: set front right back as the test leg and run the
     slow version there, to confirm it moves smoothly.

  The README (servo_test_leg/README.md) is updated for the new behaviour and
  timings.

  Ran 2 shell commands (ctrl+o to expand)

● The leg is stuck because a safety check stopped the start, not because the
  firmware hung. Two things happened:
  1. BFL (body) switched on normally. Its first pulse was at 67.0°, the angle
     its pot read, and its pot offset came out at −0.1°, so it held perfectly.
  2. FFL (femur) got its first pulse at 122.7°, the angle its pot read at that
     moment. The servo didn't hold there. It moved more than 5° away and is now
     sitting at 151° on its pot, about 28° off. After 400 ms the switch-on check
     saw this and cut all pulses: the red LED is on and the outputs are 0. TFL
     never switched on, so the whole leg is now limp. That's why it looks stuck.

  The board itself is fine: it's powered, running and idle, and the RAM values
  confirm the switch-on check fired at FFL, not the fault cut-out. The femur
  probably did move suddenly by roughly 28° at that point.

  What this shows: on the front-left femur, the pot reading and the PWM don't
  agree. A pulse for X° puts this servo where its pot reads roughly X + 20–28°.
  The front-right servos were within 2.4°. That probably also explains the first
  violent front-left move: the pulse for 87° drove the femur to its top
  position, where the pot read 107.5°. Possible causes:
  - The FL femur servo was replaced, or its horn was taken off and refitted,
    after calibration_ADC.h was measured.
  - The calibration table for servo 6 doesn't match this servo.

  The slow start depends on pot and PWM agreeing. That's why this servo still
  jumped.

  Don't press USER again while front left is selected. Every start would jump
  the femur again, by an unpredictable amount.

  How to go on:
  1. Safe now: I switch the test leg back to front right, which is verified, and
     you watch the slow version run there.
  2. Fix the front-left femur: I add a pot correction for FFL, estimated from
     the two readings we have, so its first pulse lands within a few degrees.
     Then a slow ±5° sweep measures the real pulse-to-angle relationship, and
     the firmware uses it from then on. The first switch-on may still twitch by
     a few degrees.
  3. Small improvement either way: while idle, the firmware keeps re-printing
     the reason for the last stop, so it's visible whenever someone opens the
     serial port.

  Was the front-left femur servo replaced or taken apart recently? And which
  option do you want: 1, 2, or both?

> it goes all the way up again on power on and touching blue button

● I'll make the board safe first, then find out what's wrong with the front-left
  femur.

  The femur jumps because the slow start assumes its pot reading matches its
  PWM, and on FFL it clearly doesn't. No start logic can fix that without
  knowing that servo's real pulse-to-angle relationship. So I'll flash a
  feedback-only build where the blue button can't move anything. Three more
  changes go in:
  - Pins held low from boot: all 12 servo signal pins are driven low from the
    first instructions after reset. A floating signal line can make a servo
    twitch while the board powers up or reboots.
  - Range recorder: a new r command prints the minimum and maximum each pot has
    read since the last r, so the leg can be measured by hand.
  - Last-stop reason: while idle, the firmware repeats why it last stopped every
    5 s.

       750 
       751 +/* =================== Troubleshooting program (PROGRAM_CHECK
           +) =================== */                                     
       752 +/* One servo at a time: pot check with pulses off, then the f
           +irst pulse at the                                            
       753 + * servo's own measured angle while every pot of the leg is w
           +atched at 1 ms (cut                                          
       754 + * at once if anything moves), then small slow sweeps around 
           +that position to                                             
       755 + * measure the pot offset and gain. The servo goes limp again
           + before the next one.                                        
       756 + * Neutral angles and joint limits are not used, so a wrong n
           +eutral cannot hurt. */                                       
       757 +                                                             
       758 +#define CK_AMPS (sizeof CHECK_AMPLITUDE_DEG / sizeof CHECK_AM
           +PLITUDE_DEG[0])                                              
       759 +                                                             
       760 +typedef enum { CK_NOT_RUN, CK_PASS, CK_GAIN, CK_POT_INVALID, 
           +CK_PROBE_MOVED, CK_FAULT, CK_STOPPED } ck_status_t;          
       761 +static const char *const CK_STATUS_TEXT[] = {                
       762 +    "not run", "PASS", "GAIN MISMATCH", "POT INVALID", "MOVED
           + ON FIRST PULSE", "FAULT (not following)", "stopped",        
       763 +};                                                           
       764 +                                                             
       765 +typedef struct {                                             
       766 +    ck_status_t status;                                      
       767 +    float start_deg, offset, track_max, other_max;           
       768 +    float gain[CK_AMPS];                                     
       769 +    float probe_move[JOINTS_PER_LEG];   /* pot changes when t
           +he first pulse was cut */                                    
       770 +} ck_result_t;                                               
       771 +                                                             
       772 +static uint32_t ck_next_tick, ck_next_telemetry;             
       773 +                                                             
       774 +/* Median of the last 8 complete ADC scans of servo n (about 
           +2 ms), in degrees.                                           
       775 + * Used while probing, where the 16 ms window would react too
           + late. */                                                    
       776 +static float fast_fb_deg(int n)                              
       777 +{                                                            
       778 +    uint32_t written = ADC_SCANS * 12u - __HAL_DMA_GET_COUNTE
           +R(&hdma_adc1);                                               
       779 +    int s = (int)(written / 12u) - 1;          /* last comple
           +te scan */                                                   
       780 +    uint16_t v[8];                                           
       781 +    for (int i = 0; i < 8; i++, s--) {                       
       782 +        if (s < 0) s += ADC_SCANS;                           
       783 +        v[i] = adc_buf[s][n];                                
       784 +    }                                                        
       785 +    for (int i = 1; i < 8; i++) {                            
       786 +        uint16_t x = v[i];                                   
       787 +        int k = i - 1;                                       
       788 +        while (k >= 0 && v[k] > x) { v[k + 1] = v[k]; k--; } 
       789 +        v[k + 1] = x;                                        
       790 +    }                                                        
       791 +    return adc_to_deg(n, (v[3] + v[4]) * 0.5f);              
       792 +}                                                            
       793 +                                                             
       794 +/* One 10 ms step: feedback, outputs, telemetry. False if USE
           +R or 'x' asked to stop. */                                   
       795 +static bool check_tick(void)                                 
       796 +{                                                            
       797 +    while ((int32_t)(HAL_GetTick() - ck_next_tick) < 0)      
       798 +        __WFI();                                             
       799 +    ck_next_tick += UPDATE_PERIOD_MS;                        
       800 +    uint32_t now = HAL_GetTick();                            
       801 +    char c = uart_getc();                                    
       802 +    bool pressed = button_pressed();                         
       803 +    feedback_update();                                       
       804 +    output_update();                                         
       805 +    if ((int32_t)(now - ck_next_telemetry) >= 0) {           
       806 +        ck_next_telemetry += TELEMETRY_MS;                   
       807 +        print_telemetry(now);                                
       808 +    }                                                        
       809 +    return !(pressed || c == 'x' || c == 'X');               
       810 +}                                                            
       811 +                                                             
       812 +static bool check_wait(uint32_t ms)                          
       813 +{                                                            
       814 +    uint32_t t0 = HAL_GetTick();                             
       815 +    while (HAL_GetTick() - t0 < ms)                          
       816 +        if (!check_tick()) return false;                     
       817 +    return true;                                             
       818 +}                                                            
       819 +                                                             
       820 +/* First pulse at the measured angle, watching all three pots
           + of the leg at 1 ms.                                         
       821 + * Returns false (pulses already cut) if any pot moved more t
           +han CHECK_PROBE_CUT_DEG. */                                  
       822 +static bool check_probe(int l, int j, ck_result_t *r)        
       823 +{                                                            
       824 +    float ref[JOINTS_PER_LEG];                               
       825 +    for (int k = 0; k < JOINTS_PER_LEG; k++) ref[k] = fast_fb
           +_deg(servo_of(l, k));                                        
       826 +    joint_enable(l, j);                                      
       827 +    uint32_t t0 = HAL_GetTick(), t = t0;                     
       828 +    while (t - t0 < CHECK_PROBE_MS) {                        
       829 +        bool cut = false;                                    
       830 +        for (int k = 0; k < JOINTS_PER_LEG; k++) {           
       831 +            r->probe_move[k] = fast_fb_deg(servo_of(l, k)) - 
           +ref[k];                                                      
       832 +            if (absf(r->probe_move[k]) > CHECK_PROBE_CUT_DEG)
           + cut = true;                                                 
       833 +        }                                                    
       834 +        if (cut) {                                           
       835 +            set_pulse(l, j, 0);          /* takes effect at t
           +he end of this frame */                                      
       836 +            joint_on[l][j] = false;                          
       837 +            return false;                                    
       838 +        }                                                    
       839 +        while (HAL_GetTick() == t) { }                       
       840 +        t = HAL_GetTick();                                   
       841 +    }                                                        
       842 +    return true;                                             
       843 +}                                                            
       844 +                                                             
       845 +static ck_result_t check_one(int l, int j)                   
       846 +{                                                            
       847 +    ck_result_t r;                                           
       848 +    memset(&r, 0, sizeof r);                                 
       849 +    uint8_t n = servo_of(l, j);                              
       850 +    const servo_t *s = &SERVOS[n];                           
       851 +    float other_ref[JOINTS_PER_LEG];                         
       852 +    float to[LEG_COUNT][JOINTS_PER_LEG];                     
       853 +    segment_t sg;                                            
       854 +                                                             
       855 +    uart_puts("--- ");                                       
       856 +    uart_puts(s->name);                                      
       857 +    uart_puts(" (");                                         
       858 +    uart_puts(JOINT_NAME[j]);                                
       859 +    uart_puts(", servo ");                                   
       860 +    uart_int(n, false);                                      
       861 +    uart_puts(", PWM ");                                     
       862 +    uart_puts(s->pin_name);                                  
       863 +    uart_puts(", pot ");                                     
       864 +    uart_puts(s->fb_pin);                                    
       865 +    uart_puts(") ---\r\n");                                  
       866 +    if (!check_wait(300)) { r.status = CK_STOPPED; return r; 
           +}                                                            
       867 +                                                             
       868 +    uart_puts("  pulses off: pot reads ");                   
       869 +    uart_dec1(fb_deg[n], false);                             
       870 +    uart_puts(" deg (adc ");                                 
       871 +    uart_int(fb_raw[n], false);                              
       872 +    uart_puts(", spread ");                                  
       873 +    uart_int(fb_iqr[n], false);                              
       874 +    uart_puts(")\r\n");                                      
       875 +    if (!fb_valid(n)) {                                      
       876 +        uart_puts("  pot reading invalid (out of the calibrat
           +ion range or noisy): servo not switched on\r\n");            
       877 +        r.status = CK_POT_INVALID;                           
       878 +        return r;                                            
       879 +    }                                                        
       880 +    r.start_deg = fb_deg[n];                                 
       881 +                                                             
       882 +    if (!check_probe(l, j, &r)) {                            
       883 +        uart_puts("  first pulse at ");                      
       884 +        uart_dec1(r.start_deg, false);                       
       885 +        uart_puts(" deg: pots moved");                       
       886 +        for (int k = 0; k < JOINTS_PER_LEG; k++) {           
       887 +            uart_puts(" ");                                  
       888 +            uart_puts(SERVOS[servo_of(l, k)].name);          
       889 +            uart_puts(" ");

       891 +        }

       893 +        r.status = CK_PROBE_MOVED;                           
       894 +        check_wait(500);                                     
       895 +        return r;                                            
       896 +    }                                                        
       897 +    r.offset = fb_deg[n] - out_deg[l][j];                    
       898 +    offset_deg[l][j]   = r.offset;                           
       899 +    offset_known[l][j] = true;                               
       900 +    uart_puts("  holds its first pulse at ");                
       901 +    uart_dec1(out_deg[l][j], false);                         
       902 +    uart_puts(" deg, pot offset ");                          
       903 +    uart_dec1(r.offset, true);                               
       904 +    uart_puts(" deg\r\n");                                   
       905 +    for (int k = 0; k < JOINTS_PER_LEG; k++) other_ref[k] = f
           +b_deg[servo_of(l, k)];                                       
       906 +                                                             
       907 +    for (unsigned a = 0; a < CK_AMPS; a++) {                 
       908 +        const float A = CHECK_AMPLITUDE_DEG[a];

       913 +            to[l][j] = target[t];

           +ck(), CHECK_SPEED_DEG_S);

       917 +                if (!check_tick()) { r.status = CK_STOPPED; g
           +oto off; }

       919 +                float e = absf(joint_err(l, j));

       921 +                for (int k = 0; k < JOINTS_PER_LEG; k++) {

       938 +        }

       940 +        uart_puts("  +/-");

       949 +    }

       958 +    return r;                                                
       959 +}                                                            
       960 +                                                             
       961 +static void run_check(void)                                  
       962 +{                                                            
       963 +    static ck_result_t res[LEG_COUNT][JOINTS_PER_LEG];       
       964 +    bool stopped = false;                                    
       965 +                                                             
       966 +    memset(res, 0, sizeof res);                              
       967 +    ck_next_tick = ck_next_telemetry = HAL_GetTick();        
       968 +    led(LED_BLUE, false);                                    
       969 +    led(LED_RED, false);                                     
       970 +    led(LED_GREEN, true);                                    
       971 +    uart_puts("=== servo check: one servo at a time, small sl
           +ow moves around where it is now ===\r\n");                   
       972 +                                                             
       973 +    for (int l = 0; l < LEG_COUNT && !stopped; l++) {        
       974 +        if (!leg_selected(l)) continue;                      
       975 +        for (int j = 0; j < JOINTS_PER_LEG && !stopped; j++) 
           +{                                                            
       976 +            res[l][j] = check_one(l, j);                     
       977 +            if (res[l][j].status == CK_STOPPED || !check_wait
           +(500)) stopped = true;                                       
       978 +        }                                                    
       979 +    }                                                        
       980 +    outputs_off();                                           
       981 +                                                             
       982 +    uart_puts("=== servo check summary ===\r\n");            
       983 +    bool all_pass = !stopped;                                
       984 +    for (int l = 0; l < LEG_COUNT; l++) {                    
       985 +        if (!leg_selected(l)) continue;                      
       986 +        for (int j = 0; j < JOINTS_PER_LEG; j++) {           
       987 +            const ck_result_t *r = &res[l][j];               
       988 +            uart_puts("  ");                                 
       989 +            uart_puts(SERVOS[servo_of(l, j)].name);          
       990 +            uart_puts("  ");                                 
       991 +            uart_puts(CK_STATUS_TEXT[r->status]);            
       992 +            if (r->status == CK_PASS || r->status == CK_GAIN 
           +|| r->status == CK_FAULT) {                                  
       993 +                uart_puts("  start ");                       
       994 +                uart_dec1(r->start_deg, false);              
       995 +                uart_puts("  pot offset ");                  
       996 +                uart_dec1(r->offset, true);                  
       997 +                uart_puts("  gain");                         
       998 +                for (unsigned a = 0; a < CK_AMPS; a++) {     
       999 +                    uart_puts(a ? "/" : " ");                
      1000 +                    uart_dec1(r->gain[a], false);            
      1001 +                }                                            
      1002 +                uart_puts("  tracking max ");
