● Update(servo_test_leg\README.md)
  ⎿  Added 27 lines
       2 
       3  A small test firmware for the NUCLEO-F412ZG. It moves the selec
          ted legs **slowly** through a fixed sequence of joint sweeps an
          d reads each servo's pot feedback. It runs open loop: it doesn'
          t use the Raspberry Pi or the SPI link. The feedback is used to
           start without jumps and to stop on faults, not to control the
          servos. Only the PWM pins of the selected legs are configured.
          Servos on any other leg get no signal.
       4 
       5 +## Programs                                                    
       6 +                                                               
       7 +Set `PROGRAM` at the top of `Src/main.c`:                      
       8 +                                                               
       9 +- **`PROGRAM_CHECK`** (the default) is a troubleshooting progra
         +m for any leg, including ones whose neutral angles or calibrati
         +on aren't verified. It never goes to neutral. It tests one serv
         +o at a time:                                                   
      10 +  1. With pulses off, it checks that the pot reading is in rang
         +e and steady.                                                  
      11 +  2. It sends the first pulse at the servo's own measured angle
         + and watches all three pots of the leg every 1 ms. If any pot m
         +oves more than 4°, that servo is cut at once. This catches a po
         +t/PWM mismatch, or a PWM line that drives a different servo, af
         +ter a few degrees of movement.                                 
      12 +  3. It sweeps ±5° and then ±10° around that position at 5°/s, 
         +with the fault cut-out active. This measures the pot offset, th
         +e gain (pot change ÷ commanded change, which should be about 1,
         + judged on the ±10° sweep), the tracking error, and how much th
         +e leg's other pots move.                                       
      13 +  4. The servo goes limp, and a summary is printed at the end. 
      14 +                                                               
      15 +  A full leg takes about 65 s.                                 
      16 +- **`PROGRAM_SEQUENCE`** runs the slow test sequence around neu
         +tral, described below. Use it only on legs that passed the chec
         +k and whose neutral angles are known.                          
      17 +- `MOTION_ENABLED 0` builds a feedback-only version: no pulse c
         +an be sent. Use it to read the pots while you move joints by ha
         +nd, with `r` printing the min..max range of each pot.          
      18 +                                                               
      19 +All 12 servo signal lines are driven low from the first instruc
         +tions after reset, so no servo sees a floating line. While idle
         +, the reason for the last stop is printed every 5 s.           
      20 +                                                               
      21 +### Check results, 2026-11-17                                  
      22 +                                                               
      23 +| Servo | Result |                                             
      24 +|---|---|                                                      
      25 +| BFR, FFR, TFR | Pot offset about −2.4°, tracks well (tested w
         +ith the sequence program) |                                    
      26 +| BFL | Holds its first pulse, offset −0.1° |                  
      27 +| FFL | **Pot and PWM disagree by about 20–28°.** The first pul
         +se moves the femur hard upwards. |                             
      28 +| BBR | Holds, offset −2.3°, gain 1.0 over ±10° |              
      29 +| FBR | PASS: offset −0.8°, gain 1.0 |                         
      30 +| TBR | **Pot reads about 250° (neutral is 80°)** and hardly ch
         +anges when the femur moves. Moved 4.1° on its first pulse and w
         +as cut. Check its feedback wire (PC2) and the servo. |         
      31 +                                                               
      32  ## Motion safety
      33 
      34  - **Slow, eased moves.** Every move is planned from a speed lim
          it instead of a fixed time. The joint that moves furthest peaks
           at `MAX_SPEED_DEG_S` (10°/s), and every move starts and ends a
          t zero speed.

  The TBR problem:
  - Its pot reads about 250°, while its neutral is 80° and the tibia only moves
    ±15°.
  - When the femur swept 20°, the tibia pot changed by only 1.2°. On the front
    left leg the tibia pot moved strongly when the femur moved.
  - On the first pulse it moved 4.1° in the wrong direction, and the probe cut
    it within milliseconds.

  The likely cause is the TBR feedback wire (PC2): a broken pot ground, a
  reversed pot, or a replaced servo. Under the older firmware, TBR would have
  been sent about 170° in one go. Don't run TBR, or the sequence program on this
  leg, until it's fixed.

  Checking it without power to the servos: move the back right tibia slowly by
  hand from one end to the other, then tell me. I'll read the pot's range with
  the new r command:
  - If the reading doesn't change, the feedback wire is broken.
  - If it changes in the opposite direction, the pot is reversed.

  What changed in the firmware:
  - Board state: the board now has the check program flashed. It does nothing
    until you press USER or I send s.
  - Pins from boot: all 12 servo signal lines are driven low from the first
    instructions after reset, so no servo sees a floating line.
  - Last stop reason: while idle, the reason for the last stop is printed every
    5 s.
  - Settings: PROGRAM_CHECK is the default, PROGRAM_SEQUENCE is the slow
    sequence for verified legs, and MOTION_ENABLED 0 gives a feedback-only
    build.
  - Gain check: BBR was flagged "gain mismatch" in this run because it fell
    short on the small ±5° sweep. I've changed the check to judge gain on the
    ±10° sweep only, so it would pass now.

  The README (servo_test_leg/README.md) has the results for every servo tested
  so far. The full log is at C:\Users\Julian\AppData\Local\Temp\claude\b4771f323

  So these aren't angles. The calibration math just clips them to 0° or 266°.
  When this leg was tested earlier, its pots read sensible values (BFL 65°, FFL
  123°, TFL 82°). All three reading the extremes together points at the leg's
  wiring or power, not the servos themselves:
  - the FL servo connectors are unplugged,
  - the feedback wires are disconnected,
  - or the FL leg's servo supply or ground is off.

  A pot with no ground but with supply reads near the top, like BFL and TFL. One
  with ground but no supply reads 0, like FFL. Did you unplug or rewire the
  front left leg after it slammed up?

  This is safe for now: the firmware refuses to switch on any servo whose pot
  reads outside its calibration range. So even if someone pressed start on this
  leg, nothing would move.

> do a full movement of the front right leg, slowly, to detect the mechanical
  limits, do not push the code yet

● I'll write a limit-finding program for the front right leg. I'll build it but
  not flash it, since you said not to push the code yet.

  How it will work:
  1. Start: each joint's pot must read a valid value. Then each servo gets its
     first pulse at its own measured angle, with the same fast 1 ms cut as the
     check program. All three joints then hold where they are, so the leg's
     shape stays fixed.
  2. Search: one joint at a time, the command moves slowly (5°/s, a steady
     speed) in the + direction. A limit is detected when the pot falls 3° behind
     the command for 150 ms, meaning the joint is blocked or can't follow. It
     then stops pushing at once: the command is set to where the joint actually
     is. Then it backs off 5° and returns to the start. The same happens in the
     − direction.
  3. Caps: a search never goes more than 135° from the start, or outside 5–260°.
  4. Result: the low and high limit of each joint in degrees and as pulse
     widths, the range, and whether neutral lies inside it.

         +1 << JOINT_BODY/FEMUR/TIBIA) */                                
      85 +#define LIMIT_SPEED_DEG_S   5.0f    /* constant search speed to
         +wards a limit          */                                      
      86 +#define LIMIT_LAG_DEG       3.0f    /* pot this far behind the 
         +command ...            */                                      
      87 +#define LIMIT_DETECT_MS     150u    /* ... for this long = limi
         +t (blocked)            */

         + limit                 */

      1036      uart_puts(all_pass ? "all servos PASS\r\n" : "not all ser

      1038 
      1039 +/* =================== Mechanical limit search (PROGRAM_LIMIT
           +S) =================== */                                    
      1040 +/* All joints of the leg are switched on one at a time with t
           +he safe probe and then                                       
      1041 + * hold where they are. Each joint in turn moves at LIMIT_SPE
           +ED_DEG_S until its pot                                       
      1042 + * lags LIMIT_LAG_DEG behind the command for LIMIT_DETECT_MS:
           + that is its limit (blocked,                                 
      1043 + * or it cannot follow any more). It stops pushing at once, b
           +acks off, returns to the                                     
      1044 + * start and repeats in the other direction. Angles are on th
           +e PWM/pot scale. */                                          
      1045 +                                                             
      1046 +typedef struct {                                             
      1047 +    bool     done;                                           
      1048 +    float    start;                 /* command at the start (
           +deg)                     */                                  
      1049 +    float    lim[2];                /* [0] low, [1] high: pot
           + angle at the limit       */                                 
      1050 +    float    lim_cmd[2];            /* the same limits as com
           +mands (pot - offset)      */                                 
      1051 +    bool     blocked[2];            /* true: blocked; false: 
           +travel cap reached        */                                 
      1052 +} lim_result_t;                                              
      1053 +                                                             
      1054 +enum { RUN_OK = 0, RUN_STOPPED = -1, RUN_FAULT = -2 };       
      1055 +                                                             
      1056 +/* Runs a planned segment to its end. */                     
      1057 +static int run_segment(segment_t *sg)                        
      1058 +{                                                            
      1059 +    for (;;) {                                               
      1060 +        bool done = segment_step(sg, HAL_GetTick());         
      1061 +        if (!check_tick()) return RUN_STOPPED;               
      1062 +        if (fault_detected()) return RUN_FAULT;              
      1063 +        if (done) return RUN_OK;                             
      1064 +    }                                                        
      1065 +}                                                            
      1066 +                                                             
      1067 +/* Plans and runs a move of joint (l, j) to `target` at speed
           +_deg_s; the others hold. */                                  
      1068 +static int move_joint(int l, int j, float target, float speed
           +_deg_s)                                                      
      1069 +{                                                            
      1070 +    float to[LEG_COUNT][JOINTS_PER_LEG];                     
      1071 +    segment_t sg;                                            
      1072 +    memcpy(to, cmd_deg, sizeof to);                          
      1073 +    to[l][j] = target;                                       
      1074 +    segment_plan_at(&sg, to, 200, HAL_GetTick(), speed_deg_s)
           +;                                                            
      1075 +    return run_segment(&sg);                                 
      1076 +}                                                            
      1077 +                                                             
      1078 +/* Moves joint (l, j) at constant speed in direction dir (+1/
           +-1) until it is blocked                                      
      1079 + * (returns 1) or the travel cap is reached (returns 0). *pot
           + / *cmd = where it ended. */                                 
      1080 +static int limit_search(int l, int j, int dir, float start, f
           +loat *pot, float *cmd)                                       
      1081 +{                                                            
      1082 +    const float step = LIMIT_SPEED_DEG_S * (float)UPDATE_PERI
           +OD_MS / 1000.0f;                                             
      1083 +    uint8_t  n = servo_of(l, j);                             
      1084 +    uint32_t lag_ms = 0;                                     
      1085 +                                                             
      1086 +    /* This joint is expected to lag here, so take it out of 
           +the fault cut-out while                                      
      1087 +     * searching (the other joints stay protected). */       
      1088 +    offset_known[l][j] = false;                              
      1089 +    for (;;) {                                               
      1090 +        float next = cmd_deg[l][j] + (float)dir * step;      
      1091 +        bool  cap  = absf(next - start) > LIMIT_MAX_TRAVEL_DE
           +G || next < LIMIT_MIN_DEG || next > LIMIT_MAX_DEG;           
      1092 +        if (!cap) cmd_deg[l][j] = next;                      
      1093 +        if (!check_tick()) { offset_known[l][j] = true; retur
           +n RUN_STOPPED; }                                             
      1094 +        if (fault_detected()) { offset_known[l][j] = true; re
           +turn RUN_FAULT; }                                            
      1095 +                                                             
      1096 +        float lag = (float)dir * (out_deg[l][j] + offset_deg[
           +l][j] - fb_deg[n]);                                          
      1097 +        lag_ms = lag > LIMIT_LAG_DEG ? lag_ms + UPDATE_PERIOD
           +_MS : 0;                                                     
      1098 +        if (lag_ms >= LIMIT_DETECT_MS || (cap && lag_ms == 0)
           +) {                                                          
      1099 +            *pot = fb_deg[n];                                
      1100 +            *cmd = out_deg[l][j];                            
      1101 +            /* Stop pushing at once: command the joint where 
           +it actually is. */                                           
      1102 +            cmd_deg[l][j] = out_deg[l][j] = fb_deg[n] - offse
           +t_deg[l][j];                                                 
      1103 +            offset_known[l][j] = true;                       
      1104 +            err_avg[l][j] = 0.0f;                            
      1105 +            return lag_ms >= LIMIT_DETECT_MS ? 1 : 0;        
      1106 +        }                                                    
      1107 +    }                                                        
      1108 +}                                                            
      1109 +

      1136 +static void run_limits(void)                                 
      1137 +{                                                            
      1138 +    static lim_result_t res[LEG_COUNT][JOINTS_PER_LEG];      
      1139 +    ck_result_t probe;

           +one joint at a time moves slowly to each limit ===\r\n");    
      1148 +                                                             
      1149 +    /* 1. All pots of the selected legs must be valid. */    
      1150 +    if (!check_wait(300)) rc = RUN_STOPPED;                  
      1151 +    for (int l = 0; l < LEG_COUNT && rc == RUN_OK; l++) {    
      1152 +        if (!leg_selected(l)) continue;

      1164 +    }                                                        
      1165 +                                                             
      1166 +    /* 2. Switch every joint on at its measured angle with th
           +e safe probe; they then hold. */                             
      1167 +    for (int l = 0; l < LEG_COUNT && rc == RUN_OK; l++) {    
      1168 +        if (!leg_selected(l)) continue;                      
      1169 +        for (int j = 0; j < JOINTS_PER_LEG && rc == RUN_OK; j
           +++) {                                                        
      1170 +            uint8_t n = servo_of(l, j);                      
      1171 +            memset(&probe, 0, sizeof probe);                 
      1172 +            if (!check_probe(l, j, &probe)) {                
      1173 +                uart_puts("  ");                             
      1174 +                uart_puts(SERVOS[n].name);                   
      1175 +                uart_puts(" moved on its first pulse (");    
      1176 +                uart_dec1(probe.probe_move[j], true);        
      1177 +                uart_puts(" deg): pulses cut, not starting\r\
           +n");                                                         
      1178 +                set_last_stop("limit search: servo moved on i
           +ts first pulse", SERVOS[n].name);                            
      1179 +                rc = RUN_FAULT;                              
      1180 +                break;                                       
      1181 +            }                                                
      1182 +            feedback_update();                               
      1183 +            offset_deg[l][j]   = fb_deg[n] - out_deg[l][j];  
      1184 +            offset_known[l][j] = true;                       
      1185 +            uart_puts("  ");

      1187 +            uart_puts(" on and holding at ");                
      1188 +            uart_dec1(out_deg[l][j], false);                 
      1189 +            uart_puts(" deg, pot offset ");                  
      1190 +            uart_dec1(offset_deg[l][j], true);               
      1191 +            uart_puts("\r\n");                               
      1192 +            if (!check_wait(300)) rc = RUN_STOPPED;          
      1193 +        }                                                    
      1194 +    }                                                        
      1195 +                                                             
      1196 +    /* 3. Search the limits of each joint, + then -, returnin
           +g to the start in between. */                                
      1197 +    for (int l = 0; l < LEG_COUNT && rc == RUN_OK; l++) {    
      1198 +        if (!leg_selected(l)) continue;                      
      1199 +        for (int j = 0; j < JOINTS_PER_LEG && rc == RUN_OK; j
           +++) {                                                        
      1200 +            if (!(LIMIT_JOINTS & (1u << j))) continue;       
      1201 +            lim_result_t *r = &res[l][j];                    
      1202 +            r->start = out_deg[l][j];                        
      1203 +            uart_puts("--- ");

● I've written the limit-finding program for the front right leg. It's built
  with no warnings, but not flashed, as you asked. The board is still running
  the back left check program and waiting, with no servo pulses.

  What it does once flashed and started with USER or s:
  1. Pot check: it refuses to start if any front right pot reads outside its
     valid range.
  2. Switch-on: it switches on BFR, then FFR, then TFR. Each servo starts at the
     angle its pot reads, with the 1 ms cut if a pot moves more than 4°. Then
     all three hold where they are.
  3. Limit search: one joint at a time moves at a steady 5°/s in the +
     direction. A limit is detected when the pot falls 3° behind the command for
     150 ms, meaning the joint is blocked or can't lift any further. It then
     stops pushing at once, backs off 5°, returns to the start at 10°/s, and
     repeats in the − direction.
  4. Safety: a search never goes more than 135° from the start, or outside
     5–260°. The fault cut-out keeps watching the two joints that are holding.
     USER or x cuts all pulses at any time.
  5. Result: for each joint it prints the low and high limits in degrees and
     microseconds, the range, whether each end was a real block or just the
     travel cap, and whether the neutral angle lies inside the range.

  Timing and what to expect:
  - Duration: about 1–2 minutes per joint, so 3–5 minutes for the leg.
  - Push at a limit: when a joint reaches a limit it presses against it briefly,
    about 0.6 s with the error building up to around 3°. That's a firm but
    brief push, not a slam.

  Before running it:
  - Lift the robot off the ground. Otherwise the femur or tibia will "find" the
    ground as its limit, and the leg will push against the robot's weight.
  - Check the readings first: your front right pots last read 128° / 216° /
    145°, very far from the earlier positions. If those readings are wrong, the
    start-up probe will catch it and cut, but it's better to confirm first that
    they match how the leg looks.
  - Expect a pause: the search can push towards the same direction the front
    left femur slammed into. Here it's at 5°/s with a quick release, but watch
    the first joint and stop it with USER if anything looks wrong.

  When you're ready, tell me and I'll flash it and run it, recording the log.
